  Flannel AI — Complete Codebase Overview

  What It Is

  A property management platform connecting homebuilders with homeowners. Built
  with Next.js 14 (App Router) + Supabase + OpenAI, deployed on Vercel.

  Tech Stack

  ┌────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Layer    │                      Technology                      │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Frontend   │ Next.js 14.2.6, React 18, TypeScript                 │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Styling    │ Tailwind CSS + shadcn/ui (Radix primitives)          │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ State      │ Zustand (global) + React Context (auth/user)         │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Database   │ Supabase PostgreSQL + pgvector                       │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Auth       │ Supabase OTP (magic links, passwordless)             │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ AI Chat    │ OpenAI GPT-4o via Vercel AI SDK                      │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Embeddings │ text-embedding-3-large (3072d)                       │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ OCR        │ Azure Document Intelligence + GPT-4o Vision fallback │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Email      │ SendGrid                                             │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Deploy     │ Vercel (serverless + cron)                           │
  └────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Three User Roles

  - homeowner — manages their property, tasks, files, service requests
  - builder — creates properties, invites homeowners, manages all linked
  properties
  - admin — future expansion

  ---
  Database — 17 Tables

  users, properties, to_do, task_comments, task_attachments, service_requests,
  service_request_comments, service_request_attachments,
  service_request_activity, documents_metadata, document_properties, documents
  (vector store), conversations, notifications, warranty_tickets,
  warranty_ticket_tasks, ticket_trades, ticket_categories

  Key Data Flows

  1. Auth: Magic link OTP → /auth/callback → profile upsert → role-based
  dashboard
  2. Document Upload: PDF → Supabase Storage → /api/process-pdf → 5-phase
  pipeline → 3-layer chunking → vector embeddings
  3. AI Chat: User query → BM25 + semantic search + GPT-4o-mini re-ranking →
  context injection → GPT-4o streaming response
  4. Service Requests: Homeowner creates → builder notified via email → status
  lifecycle (new→in_review→scheduled→completed→closed)
  5. Tasks: CRUD + recurring (daily/weekly/monthly) → cron generates instances +
   sends reminders

  Supabase Client Architecture

  - Browser client (lib/supabase/client.ts): createBrowserClient for React
  components
  - Server client (lib/supabase/server.ts): createServerClient with cookie
  access for server components/actions
  - Admin client (lib/supabase/admin.ts): Service role key, bypasses RLS — used
  for PDF processing, property assignment, activity logs
  - Middleware (lib/supabase/middleware.ts): Refreshes sessions, redirects
  unauthenticated users

Flannel AI - Claude Code Context Document                                       
                                                                                
## Project Overview                                                             
                                                                                
Flannel AI is a property management platform for homebuilders and homeowners.   
Builders provide this AI assistant to their homeowners instead of giving them a 
 physical binder of warranties, manuals, permits, and other documents that come 
 with a new home. The homeowner can then ask questions about their property     
documents through an AI-powered chat assistant that uses RAG                    
(Retrieval-Augmented Generation).                                               
                                                                                
The platform also supports shared task management, service request tracking     
(with a Kanban workflow), document management with folder organization, and     
role-based dashboards for builders and homeowners.                              
                                                                                
**Production URL:** https://flannel.ai/                                         
**Vercel Deployment:** flannel-ai.vercel.app                                    
**Client:** Scott Ryerson (Founder), Muid Abdullah (Tech Lead)                  
                                                                                
---                                                                             
                                                                                
## Tech Stack (Complete)                                                        
                                                                                
| Layer | Technology | Details |                                                
|-------|-----------|---------|                                                 
| Frontend | Next.js 14 (App Router), React 18, TypeScript | |                  
| Styling / UI | Tailwind CSS, shadcn/ui (Radix primitives), Framer Motion,     
Lucide icons | |                                                                
| Backend / API | Next.js API Routes (serverless functions) | |                 
| Database | Supabase (PostgreSQL) with pgvector extension | uuid-ossp and      
vector extensions enabled |                                                     
| Auth | Supabase Auth | Magic links, OTP, fully passwordless |                 
| File Storage | Supabase Storage | 4 buckets: Documents (50MB), task-files     
(10MB), service-request-files (10MB), avatars (2MB public) |                    
| AI / Chat | OpenAI GPT-4o (gpt-4o-2024-08-06) via Vercel AI SDK | |           
| Re-ranking | OpenAI GPT-4o-mini | Used in Stage 3 of hybrid retrieval |       
| Embeddings | OpenAI text-embedding-3-large | 3072 dimensions |                
| Document OCR | Azure Document Intelligence | East US region, prebuilt-layout  
model, API 2023-10-31-preview, $0.01/page, 4MB file limit |                     
| Vision Fallback | OpenAI GPT-4o Vision | For scanned pages, floor plans,      
tables |                                                                        
| Email | SendGrid (@sendgrid/mail) | From app@flannel.ai |                     
| Deployment | Vercel | With cron jobs at 9:00 AM UTC daily |                   
| State Management | Zustand (global), React Context (auth/user) | |            
| Drag & Drop | dnd-kit | For Kanban boards |

3. `to_do` - Tasks with CRUD, subtasks, recurring support                       
4. `task_comments` - Comments on tasks                                          
5. `task_attachments` - File attachments on tasks

styling, and shadcn/ui components. Follow existing patterns in the codebase.    
                                                                                
---                                                                             
                                                                                
## USEFUL CONTEXT FOR DEBUGGING                                                 
                                                                                
### Why the Wrong Document Gets Retrieved                                       
                                                                                
The property documents for a homeowner include multiple warranties, manuals,    
and permits. Many of these documents share similar language:                    
- Faucet warranty and kitchen sink warranty both talk about "warranty,"         
"coverage," "plumbing," "installation," "manufacturer"                          
- Multiple appliance manuals share similar structure (safety warnings,          
installation steps, maintenance schedules)                                      
- Permit documents share legal/regulatory language                              
                                                                                
With text-embedding-3-large, these documents produce vectors that are very      
close in the embedding space. At a 0.08 similarity threshold, the system cannot 
 distinguish between them. This is the fundamental retrieval precision problem. 
                                                                                
### Why Conversation History Causes "Sticky" Wrong Results                      
                                                                                
The `conversations` table stores full chat history as JSONB arrays. When the    
user asks a follow-up question, the previous messages (including the AI's wrong 
 answer citing the wrong document) likely get included in the retrieval         
context. This means:                                                            
1. User asks about faucet warranty                                              
2. System incorrectly retrieves kitchen sink warranty chunks                    
3. AI responds with kitchen sink warranty info                                  
4. On the next question, the conversation history now contains "kitchen sink    
warranty" text                                                                  
5. This text gets embedded or keyword-matched, reinforcing the kitchen sink     
document in retrieval                                                           
6. The wrong document becomes "stuck" in the conversation                       
                                                                                
The fix is query rewriting: extract the user's current standalone question from 
 the conversation context before running retrieval. The conversation history    
should inform the LLM response but should NOT be passed into the vector/BM25    
search.                                                                         
                                                                                
### About the "Open Document" Requirement                                       
                                                                                
The current system treats document opening as an explicit user action. The      
system prompt instructs the AI to only open files when explicitly requested.    
This was likely implemented as a workaround for the retrieval precision         
problem: if the system cannot figure out which document to use automatically,   
forcing the user to specify the document guarantees correct scoping. Once       
retrieval is fixed to automatically identify the right document, this           
workaround can be removed. do web search if needed and lets begin before        
pushing to prod we will test on locally those mf didn't provided env variables  
how could I test??

     Flannel AI - Bug Fix Implementation Plan

     Context

     Flannel AI is a property management RAG platform. Two confirmed bugs need
     fixing:

     Bug 1 - Wrong Document Retrieved: When a homeowner asks "what does my
     warranty cover?" without manually selecting a document, the system pools
     ALL chunks from ALL documents and returns results from the wrong document
     (e.g., kitchen sink warranty instead of faucet warranty). Users are forced
     to manually "open" a document before chatting.

     Bug 2 - Images Not Captured: The document processing pipeline has Phase 4
     (visual processing) COMMENTED OUT in pipeline.ts:126-133. Images in PDFs
     are never extracted or embedded, so the AI can't answer image-related
     questions. Client approved migrating to Gemini Embedding 2 for native
     multimodal support.

     Testing constraint: No env variables available for local testing yet
     (client providing keys soon). Changes will be verified by code review, then
      tested on Vercel test deployment.

     Implementation order: Phase 1 first (document routing with existing OpenAI
     embeddings). Verify it works. Then Phase 2 (Gemini migration) once API key
     is provided.

     ---
     Phase 1: Fix Document Routing (Bug 1)

     Do this FIRST. Verify it works before touching embeddings.

     1.1 Add source_document_id and document_title to chunk metadata

     Why: Chunks currently have NO document-level identifier. Can't filter or
     attribute chunks to their source document.

     Files to modify:

     components/modals/UploadDropzone.tsx (line 184-188)
     - Add documentId: metadata.id to the JSON body sent to /api/process-pdf

     app/api/process-pdf/route.ts (line 21, 130-139)
     - Destructure documentId from request body
     - If not provided, resolve from filePath via documents_metadata query
     (backward compat)
     - In toDocuments(), add source_document_id: resolvedDocumentId and
     document_title: title to chunk metadata

     lib/document-ai/types.ts
     - Add source_document_id?: number and document_title?: string to
     EnhancedChunkMetadata

     1.2 Backfill existing chunks

     New SQL migration: sql/BACKFILL_CHUNK_METADATA.sql

     Iterates through documents_metadata records, collects all chunk IDs from
     metadata.layers.A/B/C.chunk_ids and legacy metadata.chunk_ids, then updates
      each chunk's JSONB metadata with source_document_id and document_title.

     Also add index:
     CREATE INDEX idx_documents_source_doc_id
     ON documents ((metadata->>'source_document_id')::int);

     1.3 Implement document routing function

     File: actions/chat.ts (new function near line 1113)

     async function routeQueryToDocuments(
       userQuery: string, userId: string
     ): Promise<{ documents: Array<{id, title, score, reason}>; confidence:
     'high'|'medium'|'low' }>

     Two-stage approach:
     - Stage A (fast, ~5ms): Keyword matching — tokenize query, compare against
     document titles and metadata.section_headings from all accessible
     documents. E.g., "hansgrohe faucet warranty" → high-confidence match to
     document titled "Hansgrohe Faucet Warranty".
     - Stage B (only if Stage A is inconclusive, ~300ms): Use gpt-4o-mini with
     structured prompt listing all documents + their section headings, return
     top 1-3 document IDs.

     1.4 Integrate routing into chat flow

     File: actions/chat.ts (lines 2460-2504)

     Replace the current "pool ALL chunks" cross-document search with:

     isDocumentQuestion detected
       → routeQueryToDocuments(userInput, userId)
       → if confidence high/medium:
           use only routed document(s)' chunk IDs
       → if confidence low:
           fall back to all documents (current behavior)
           OR ask user to clarify which document

     Move routing AFTER query rewriting (line 2582) so the rewritten standalone
     query is used for routing, not the raw pronoun-laden follow-up.

     1.5 Remove "select a document first" fallback

     File: actions/chat.ts (lines 3305-3378)

     The else branch looksLikeApplianceQuestion currently tells users to select
     a document. Replace with:
     - Attempt document routing automatically
     - If routing finds a match → proceed to RAG retrieval (call the same
     retrieval logic as the document branch)
     - If no match found + user has documents → ask clarifying question ("Which
     document are you asking about?")
     - If no documents at all → suggest uploading

     1.6 Add document attribution to context

     File: actions/chat.ts (lines 2994-3001, 3114-3120)

     - In chunk text formatting, include (from: "Document Title") per chunk
     - In system prompt, list which documents were searched
     - Add instruction: "When answering, mention which document the information
     comes from"

     1.7 New SQL function for document-filtered search

     File: sql/IMPROVE_VECTOR_SEARCH.sql (add new function)

     CREATE FUNCTION match_documents_by_source(
       query_embedding_text text,
       filter_user_id text,
       filter_source_document_ids int[],  -- documents_metadata.id values
       match_count int DEFAULT 50,
       similarity_threshold float DEFAULT 0.0
     )

     More efficient than passing hundreds of chunk IDs — filters by
     (metadata->>'source_document_id')::int = ANY(filter_source_document_ids).

     ---
     Phase 2: Enable Image Extraction + Gemini Migration (Bug 2)

     Do this AFTER Phase 1 is verified working.

     2.1 Install Gemini SDK

     File: package.json
     - Add "@google/generative-ai": "^0.24.0"

     Environment: Add GOOGLE_AI_API_KEY to .env.local and Vercel

     2.2 Create Gemini embedding module

     New file: lib/embeddings/gemini-embeddings.ts

     Functions:
     - embedText(text: string): Promise<number[]> — 3072d text embedding
     - embedImage(imageBase64: string, mimeType: string): Promise<number[]> —
     3072d image embedding
     - embedBatch(items: TextOrImage[]): Promise<number[][]> — batch with rate
     limiting

     Model: gemini-embedding-2-preview, 3072 dimensions (same as current OpenAI)

     2.3 Enable Phase 4 visual processing

     File: lib/document-ai/pipeline.ts (lines 126-133)

     Uncomment and implement processVisualsFromPdf():
     1. Use analysis.pages to identify pages with images
     2. Call existing convertPdfToImages() for those pages
     3. Send page images to existing processScannedPage() /
     processVisualsInBatch() for GPT-4o Vision descriptions
     4. Return both VisualContent[] descriptions AND raw base64 images

     Also fix line 93 in process-pdf/route.ts:
     Change vlmEnabled: analysis.documentType === 'scanned' && 
     analysis.hasImages to vlmEnabled: analysis.hasImages || 
     analysis.hasFloorPlans — enable for ALL PDFs with images.

     File: lib/document-ai/types.ts
     - Add rawImageBase64?: string to VisualContent
     - Add rawPageImages?: Map<number, string> to ProcessingResult

     2.4 Replace OpenAI embeddings with Gemini in process-pdf

     File: app/api/process-pdf/route.ts (lines 150-189)

     - Remove OpenAIEmbeddings and SupabaseVectorStore usage
     - Use new embedBatch() for text chunks
     - Use embedImage() for image chunks
     - Insert directly into documents table via Supabase client
     - For each visual with rawImageBase64, create an additional image chunk
     embedded directly by Gemini

     2.5 Replace OpenAI embeddings with Gemini in chat retrieval

     File: actions/chat.ts

     Replace all embed() / embedMany() calls (using @ai-sdk/openai) with Gemini
     embedText():
     - fastPathRetrieval() (query embedding)
     - hybridRetrieval3Tier() (query variant embeddings)
     - hybridRetrieval() (query embedding)

     2.6 Create re-embedding endpoint

     New file: app/api/re-embed-all/route.ts

     - POST endpoint, accepts { userId?, documentId?, cursor? }
     - Processes one document at a time (batch of 25 chunks)
     - Re-generates all embeddings with Gemini
     - Updates documents.embedding column
     - Tracks progress in documents_metadata.processing_status
     - Cursor-based pagination for large collections
     - maxDuration: 300 for Vercel Pro

     2.7 Recalibrate similarity thresholds

     File: actions/chat.ts (lines 22-32, RETRIEVAL_CONFIG)

     After re-embedding, the similarity score distribution will shift.
     Thresholds need tuning:
     - Start with existing values (0.30 default)
     - Add logging to track score distributions
     - Adjust based on observed retrieval quality

● Update(actions/chat.ts)
  ⎿  Added 175 lines
      1155    return uniqueFiles;
      1156  }
      1157  
      1158 +/**                                                          
      1159 + * Document Routing — identifies which document(s) are most r
           +elevant to a query                                           
      1160 + * BEFORE running expensive vector/BM25 search.              
      1161 + *                                                           
      1162 + * Stage A: Fast keyword matching against document titles and
           + section headings (~5ms)                                     
      1163 + * Stage B: LLM-based routing via gpt-4o-mini if Stage A is i
           +nconclusive (~300ms)                                         
      1164 + */                                                          
      1165 +async function routeQueryToDocuments(                        
      1166 +  userQuery: string,                                         
      1167 +  userId: string                                             
      1168 +): Promise<{                                                 
      1169 +  documents: Array<{ id: number; title: string; score: number
           +; reason: string }>;                                         
      1170 +  confidence: 'high' | 'medium' | 'low';                     
      1171 +}> {                                                         
      1172 +  const startTime = Date.now();                              
      1173 +  const allDocs = await getAllAccessibleDocuments(userId, "id
           +, title, metadata");                                         
      1174 +                                                             
      1175 +  if (!allDocs || allDocs.length === 0) {                    
      1176 +    return { documents: [], confidence: 'low' };             
      1177 +  }                                                          
      1178 +                                                             
      1179 +  // If user only has 1 document, route to it directly       
      1180 +  if (allDocs.length === 1) {                                
      1181 +    const doc = allDocs[0] as any;                           
      1182 +    return {                                                 
      1183 +      documents: [{ id: doc.id, title: doc.title || '', score
           +: 10, reason: 'only document' }],                            
      1184 +      confidence: 'high',                                    
      1185 +    };                                                       
      1186 +  }                                                          
      1187 +                                                             
      1188 +  // ── Stage A: Keyword matching against titles + section he
           +adings ──                                                    
      1189 +  const queryLower = userQuery.toLowerCase();                
      1190 +  const queryTokens = queryLower                             
      1191 +    .replace(/[^\w\s-]/g, ' ')                               
      1192 +    .split(/\s+/)                                            
      1193 +    .filter(t => t.length > 2 && !STOPWORDS.has(t))          
      1194 +    .map(stem);                                              
      1195 +                                                             
      1196 +  const scored = allDocs.map((doc: any) => {                 
      1197 +    const title = (doc.title || '').toLowerCase();           
      1198 +    const sectionHeadings: string[] = (doc.metadata as any)?.
           +section_headings || [];                                      
      1199 +    const allSearchableText = [                              
      1200 +      title,                                                 
      1201 +      ...sectionHeadings.map((h: string) => h.toLowerCase()),
      1202 +    ].join(' ');                                             
      1203 +                                                             
      1204 +    const searchTokens = allSearchableText                   
      1205 +      .replace(/[^\w\s-]/g, ' ')                             
      1206 +      .split(/\s+/)                                          
      1207 +      .filter(t => t.length > 2)                             
      1208 +      .map(stem);                                            
      1209 +                                                             
      1210 +    // Score: how many query tokens appear in this document's
           + title/sections                                              
      1211 +    let score = 0;                                           
      1212 +    let matchedTerms: string[] = [];                         
      1213 +    for (const qt of queryTokens) {                          
      1214 +      // Exact title substring match (higher weight)         
      1215 +      if (title.includes(qt)) {                              
      1216 +        score += 3;                                          
      1217 +        matchedTerms.push(qt + '(title)');                   
      1218 +      }                                                      
      1219 +      // Token match in title or sections                    
      1220 +      else if (searchTokens.includes(qt)) {                  
      1221 +        score += 1;                                          
      1222 +        matchedTerms.push(qt);                               
      1223 +      }                                                      
      1224 +    }                                                        
      1225 +                                                             
      1226 +    // Bonus: direct brand/product name match in title       
      1227 +    // Check if multi-word segments of the query appear in th
           +e title                                                      
      1228 +    const queryWords = queryLower.split(/\s+/);              
      1229 +    for (let len = Math.min(4, queryWords.length); len >= 2; 
           +len--) {                                                     
      1230 +      for (let i = 0; i <= queryWords.length - len; i++) {   
      1231 +        const phrase = queryWords.slice(i, i + len).join(' ')
           +;                                                            
      1232 +        if (title.includes(phrase) && phrase.length > 4) {   
      1233 +          score += len * 2;                                  
      1234 +          matchedTerms.push(`"${phrase}"(phrase)`);          
      1235 +          break;                                             
      1236 +        }                                                    
      1237 +      }                                                      
      1238 +    }                                                        
      1239 +                                                             
      1240 +    return {                                                 
      1241 +      id: doc.id,                                            
      1242 +      title: doc.title || '',                                
      1243 +      score,                                                 
      1244 +      reason: matchedTerms.length > 0 ? `matched: ${matchedTe
           +rms.join(', ')}` : 'no keyword match',                       
      1245 +    };                                                       
      1246 +  });                                                        
      1247 +                                                             
      1248 +  // Sort by score descending                                
      1249 +  scored.sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);                  
      1250 +                                                             
      1251 +  const topScore = scored[0].score;                          
      1252 +  const secondScore = scored.length > 1 ? scored[1].score : 0
           +;                                                            
      1253 +                                                             
      1254 +  // Strong single match                                     
      1255 +  if (topScore >= 4 && topScore > secondScore * 1.5) {       
      1256 +    console.log(`🗺️ Stage A routing (${Date.now() - startTime
           +}ms): high confidence → "${scored[0].title}" (score: ${topSco
           +re})`);                                                      
      1257 +    return {                                                 
      1258 +      documents: [scored[0]],                                
      1259 +      confidence: 'high',                                    
      1260 +    };                                                       
      1261 +  }                                                          
      1262 +                                                             
      1263 +  // Multiple reasonable matches                             
      1264 +  if (topScore >= 3) {                                       
      1265 +    const candidates = scored.filter(s => s.score >= topScore

    Coverage: Hansgrohe, Inc. warrants to the original consumer purchaser that  
Hansgrohe and AXOR products will be free from defects in material and           
workmanship for as long as the original consumer purchaser owns both the        
product and the home in which the product was originally installed. For         
commercial purchasers, the warranty period is one year for Hansgrohe products   
and five years for AXOR products from the date of purchase. Products in the     
Rubbed Bronze, Matte Black, and Matte White finishes have a warranty period of  
three years from the date of purchase. This warranty is non-transferable.       
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
    What Hansgrohe Will Do: Hansgrohe, Inc., at its option, will replace any    
product or part of the product that proves defective in workmanship and/or      
material under normal installation, use, service, and maintenance. If a         
replacement part or product cannot be provided and repair is not practical or   
cannot be made in a timely fashion, Hansgrohe, Inc. may elect to refund the     
purchase price in exchange for the return of the defective product.             
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
    Exclusions: The warranty does not cover products purchased from             
unauthorized sellers, labor and other expenses for disconnection,               
deinstallation, or return of the product for warranty service, or for           
installation or reinstallation of the product. It also excludes damage          
resulting from reasonable wear and tear, outdoor use, misuse, freezing water,   
excessive water pressure, pipe corrosion, abuse, neglect, or improper           
installation, maintenance, or repair. Additionally, it does not cover           
accessories, connected materials, or products not manufactured by Hansgrohe,    
Inc., products sold for display purposes, or products purchased from            
unauthorized sellers.

❯ that tech lead mf asking me for a short video showing how i fixed it shortly

● Here's your script and screen flow:

  ---
  Video Script (~3-4 minutes)

  Screen 1: Show the problem (browser - chat page)

  ▎ "So the issue was — when a homeowner asks a question like 'what does my 
  warranty cover' without manually selecting a document first, the system either
   tells them to select a document or pulls from the wrong one. If they have a 
  faucet warranty and a sink warranty, it can't tell them apart because all 
  chunks from all documents get pooled together with no way to distinguish which
   document they belong to."

  Screen 2: Show actions/chat.ts — the routeQueryToDocuments function

  ▎ "The fix is a document routing layer that runs before the vector search. 
  It's a two-stage approach. Stage A does fast keyword matching — it tokenizes 
  the query, compares against document titles and section headings. So if 
  someone says 'Hansgrohe warranty', it instantly matches the Hansgrohe 
  document. Stage B is a fallback — if keywords are inconclusive, it calls 
  gpt-4o-mini with all the document titles and section headings to pick the 
  right one. This adds maybe 300ms."

  Screen 3: Show actions/chat.ts — the integration block (where routing replaces
   the old cross-document search)

  ▎ "Before, when no file was selected, it just pulled all chunk IDs from every 
  document and searched them all together. Now it calls the routing function 
  first, gets back the matched documents with a confidence score, and only 
  searches those specific documents' chunks. If it's ambiguous — like the user 
  just says 'warranty' and has three warranty docs — it asks them to clarify 
  instead of guessing wrong."

  Screen 4: Show actions/chat.ts — the old else branch (now replaced)

  ▎ "There was also this else branch that caught appliance questions and just 
  said 'please select a document first'. That's gone. Now it attempts routing 
  automatically — if it finds a match, it searches that document and answers 
  directly."

  Screen 5: Show app/api/process-pdf/route.ts — the toDocuments function

  ▎ "For this to work, every chunk needs to know which document it came from. I 
  added source_document_id and document_title to the chunk metadata during 
  processing. New documents get this automatically. For existing chunks, there's
   a backfill script."

  Screen 6: Show components/modals/UploadDropzone.tsx — the documentId addition

  ▎ "One line change here — the upload flow now passes the document metadata ID 
  to the processing endpoint so it gets stamped on every chunk."

  Screen 7: Show sql/BACKFILL_CHUNK_METADATA.sql

  ▎ "This is the SQL migration — it iterates through all existing documents, 
  collects their chunk IDs, and stamps each chunk with the source document ID 
  and title. Also adds a new match_documents_by_source function for filtered 
  vector search and an index for performance."

  Screen 8: Show the demo results (browser - chat)

  ▎ "Here's the result. 'What does my Hansgrohe warranty cover' — routes to the 
  correct document, answers from it. 'What is not covered under my Newport Brass
   warranty' — routes to Newport Brass, correct answer. 'What does my warranty 
  cover' — ambiguous, lists all three docs and asks which one. That's exactly 
  what we want."

  Wrap up

  ▎ "All changes are on the fix/document-routing-auto-retrieval branch. Five 
  files changed, one new SQL migration. No changes to the tech stack, just a 
  routing layer on top of the existing retrieval."

● Good point. Current chunk sizes are small — leftover from OpenAI's limits:

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
  │    Layer     │ Current Size │     Chunks (2 docs)      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ A (Semantic) │ 1000 chars   │ ~73                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ B (Keyword)  │ 1500 chars   │ ~72                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ C (Section)  │ 4000 chars   │ ~42                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ Total        │              │ ~187 per doc, 1322 total │
  └──────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

  Gemini Embedding 2 supports 8192 tokens (~32K chars) — 4x more than OpenAI. We
   can increase chunk sizes significantly, which means fewer chunks, fewer API
  calls, and less rate limiting.

  Recommended new sizes:

  ┌──────────────┬────────────┬───────────────────┐
  │    Layer     │  New Size  │     Reduction     │
  ├──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ A (Semantic) │ 2000 chars │ ~50% fewer chunks │
  ├──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ B (Keyword)  │ 4000 chars │ ~60% fewer        │
  ├──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ C (Section)  │ 8000 chars │ ~50% fewer        │
  └──────────────┴────────────┴───────────────────┘

  That would cut total chunks from ~1322 to ~500-600. Want me to update the
  chunking config and re-process?

  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

❯ does this gonna give us reference to images or etc... while doing chat??      

● Not automatically — here's what Gemini multimodal embedding actually does vs
  what we still need:

  What Gemini Embedding 2 gives us:
  - We can embed an image and a text query into the same vector space
  - So if someone asks "show me the installation diagram", the vector search CAN
   find the image chunk because they're in the same embedding space
  - This is the search/retrieval part — finding the right image

  What we still need to build to actually show images in chat:

  1. Extract images from PDFs — the pipeline's Phase 4 is still TODO. We need to
   pull actual images out of the PDF during processing
  2. Store images — save extracted images to Supabase Storage (the Documents
  bucket)
  3. Create image chunks — each image becomes a chunk with its base64 embedded
  by Gemini + a storage URL in metadata
  4. Chat UI rendering — when the AI references an image chunk, the chat needs
  to display it (like an inline image or a clickable thumbnail)

  Right now even with Gemini, the chat would only return the text description of
   what's in an image (from GPT-4o Vision in Phase 4), not the actual image
  itself.

  To get actual image references in chat, we'd need to:
  - Extract + store images during PDF processing
  - Tag those chunks with image_url in metadata
  - Update the chat response to include ![image](url) markdown when referencing
  image chunks

  Want me to build that image extraction + storage pipeline too, or focus on
  getting the Gemini text re-embedding working first once billing is enabled?

● Line 99: hasImages = pagesWithImages > 0 || scannedPages > 0. So it depends on
   p.hasImages from analyzePages. The problem is pdf-parse is a text-only parser
   — it cannot detect images in PDFs. It only extracts text. So hasImages is
  always false for native PDFs with embedded images.

  The fix is simple — for multimodal embedding, we should always extract page
  images regardless of what the text-based analyzer says. Let me fix this in
  process-pdf:

● Update(app/api/process-pdf/route.ts)

● The issue is that pdfjs-dist needs a canvas implementation for server-side
  rendering, and the worker file can't be found in Next.js serverless bundles.
  The cleanest fix is to use sharp (already in the project) with a proper worker
   setup, or use the legacy Node.js build of pdfjs-dist. Let me check the
  current pdf-to-image.ts fully and fix it:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The problem is twofold: the worker file can't be resolved, AND it tries to use
   canvas npm package which isn't installed. The proper fix for Next.js
  serverless is to use the legacy Node.js build of pdfjs-dist with the worker
  disabled, and use sharp (already installed) instead of canvas for image
  rendering. Let me rewrite this properly:

● Wait — the white pixel approach won't capture actual PDF content. The real fix
   is we don't need to render pages as pixel-perfect images. Gemini can embed
  PDFs directly, and for image extraction what we really need is to just send 
  each page as a rendered image to Gemini. Let me use a simpler approach — sharp
   can't render PDFs, but we can use pdf-parse to detect pages and send the raw
  PDF pages to Gemini's multimodal embedding directly (it accepts PDFs up to 6
  pages per request).

  Actually, the simplest working approach: install canvas package which is the
  standard way pdfjs renders on Node.js:

❯ Files API                                                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
Gemini can handle various types of input data, including text, images, and      
audio, at the same time.                                                        
                                                                                
This guide shows you how to work with media files using the Files API. The      
basic operations are the same for audio files, images, videos, documents, and   
other supported file types.                                                     
                                                                                
For file prompting guidance, check out the File prompt guide section.           
                                                                                
Upload a file                                                                   
You can use the Files API to upload a media file. Always use the Files API when 
 the total request size (including the files, text prompt, system instructions, 
 etc.) is larger than 100 MB. For PDF files, the limit is 50 MB.                
                                                                                
The following code uploads a file and then uses the file in a call to           
generateContent.                                                                
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
Go                                                                              
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import {                                                                        
  GoogleGenAI,                                                                  
  createUserContent,                                                            
  createPartFromUri,                                                            
} from "@google/genai";                                                         
                                                                                
const ai = new GoogleGenAI({});                                                 
                                                                                
async function main() {                                                         
  const myfile = await ai.files.upload({                                        
    file: "path/to/sample.mp3",                                                 
    config: { mimeType: "audio/mpeg" },                                         
  });                                                                           
                                                                                
  const response = await ai.models.generateContent({                            
    model: "gemini-3-flash-preview",                                            
    contents: createUserContent([                                               
      createPartFromUri(myfile.uri, myfile.mimeType),                           
      "Describe this audio clip",                                               
    ]),                                                                         
  });                                                                           
  console.log(response.text);                                                   
}                                                                               
                                                                                
await main();                                                                   
Get metadata for a file                                                         
You can verify that the API successfully stored the uploaded file and get its   
metadata by calling files.get.                                                  
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
Go                                                                              
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import {                                                                        
  GoogleGenAI,                                                                  
} from "@google/genai";                                                         
                                                                                
const ai = new GoogleGenAI({});                                                 
                                                                                
async function main() {                                                         
  const myfile = await ai.files.upload({                                        
    file: "path/to/sample.mp3",                                                 
    config: { mimeType: "audio/mpeg" },                                         
  });                                                                           
                                                                                
  const fileName = myfile.name;                                                 
  const fetchedFile = await ai.files.get({ name: fileName });                   
  console.log(fetchedFile);                                                     
}                                                                               
                                                                                
await main();                                                                   
List uploaded files                                                             
The following code gets a list of all the files uploaded:                       
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
Go                                                                              
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import {                                                                        
  GoogleGenAI,                                                                  
} from "@google/genai";                                                         
                                                                                
const ai = new GoogleGenAI({});                                                 
                                                                                
async function main() {                                                         
  const listResponse = await ai.files.list({ config: { pageSize: 10 } });       
  for await (const file of listResponse) {                                      
    console.log(file.name);                                                     
  }                                                                             
}                                                                               
                                                                                
await main();                                                                   
Delete uploaded files                                                           
Files are automatically deleted after 48 hours. You can also manually delete an 
 uploaded file:                                                                 
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
Go                                                                              
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import {                                                                        
  GoogleGenAI,

Embedding audio                                                                 
The following example shows how to embed an audio file using                    
gemini-embedding-2-preview.                                                     
                                                                                
Audio files can be provided as inline data or as uploaded files through the     
Files API.                                                                      
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import { GoogleGenAI } from "@google/genai";                                    
import * as fs from "node:fs";                                                  
                                                                                
async function main() {                                                         
    const ai = new GoogleGenAI({});                                             
                                                                                
    const audioBase64 = fs.readFileSync("example.mp3", { encoding: "base64" }); 
                                                                                
    const response = await ai.models.embedContent({                             
        model: 'gemini-embedding-2-preview',                                    
        contents: [{                                                            
            inlineData: {                                                       
                mimeType: 'audio/mpeg',                                         
                data: audioBase64,                                              
            },                                                                  
        }],                                                                     
    });                                                                         
                                                                                
    console.log(response.embeddings);                                           
}                                                                               
                                                                                
main();                                                                         
Embedding video                                                                 
The following example shows how to embed a video using                          
gemini-embedding-2-preview.                                                     
                                                                                
Videos can be provided as inline data or as uploaded files through the Files    
API.                                                                            
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import { GoogleGenAI } from "@google/genai";                                    
import * as fs from "node:fs";                                                  
                                                                                
async function main() {                                                         
    const ai = new GoogleGenAI({});                                             
                                                                                
    const videoBase64 = fs.readFileSync("example.mp4", { encoding: "base64" }); 
                                                                                
    const response = await ai.models.embedContent({                             
        model: 'gemini-embedding-2-preview',                                    
        contents: [{                                                            
            inlineData: {                                                       
                mimeType: 'video/mp4',                                          
                data: videoBase64,                                              
            },                                                                  
        }],                                                                     
    });                                                                         
                                                                                
    console.log(response.embeddings);                                           
}                                                                               
                                                                                
main();                                                                         
If you need to embed videos >128 seconds, you can chunk the video into          
overlapping segments and embed those chunks individually.                       
                                                                                
Embedding documents                                                             
documents in PDF format can be embedded directly. The model processes the       
visual and text content of each page.                                           
                                                                                
PDFs can be provided as inline data or as uploaded files through the Files API. 
                                                                                
Python                                                                          
JavaScript                                                                      
REST                                                                            
                                                                                
import { GoogleGenAI } from "@google/genai";                                    
import * as fs from "node:fs";                                                  
                                                                                
async function main() {                                                         
    const ai = new GoogleGenAI({});                                             
                                                                                
    const pdfBase64 = fs.readFileSync("example.pdf", { encoding: "base64" });   
                                                                                
    const response = await ai.models.embedContent({                             
        model: 'gemini-embedding-2-preview',                                    
        contents: [{                                                            
            inlineData: {                                                       
                mimeType: 'application/pdf',                                    
                data: pdfBase64,                                                
            },                                                                  
        }],                                                                     
    });                                                                         
                                                                                
    console.log(response.embeddings);                                           
}                                                                               
                                                                                
main();

● User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/warm-snacking-adleman.md · /plan to edit
     Flannel AI — Maintenance Alert System

     Context

     Scott wants an automated maintenance alert system. When a homeowner is
     assigned to a property, a clock starts. Based on manufacturer-recommended
     maintenance schedules (HVAC filters every 1-3 months, gutters biannually,
     water heater annually, etc.), the system sends email alerts BEFORE
     maintenance is due and auto-creates tasks the homeowner can track and mark
     complete.

     The existing codebase already has: daily cron (9 AM UTC), SendGrid email
     service, recurring tasks, warranty ticket task generation, notification
     bell, and property claiming flow (the clock start trigger).

     ---
     What We're Building

     1. Maintenance schedule definitions — predefined schedules for common home
     maintenance items
     2. Auto-assignment on property claim — when homeowner claims property,
     generate all maintenance tasks
     3. Email alerts — send reminders X days before maintenance is due
     4. Task integration — maintenance items appear as tasks the homeowner can
     view/complete

     ---
     Implementation

     Step 1: Database — maintenance_schedules table

     New table storing the predefined maintenance schedule templates.

     CREATE TABLE public.maintenance_schedules (
       id serial PRIMARY KEY,
       category text NOT NULL,           -- 'monthly', 'quarterly', 'biannual', 
     'annual', '3-5_years', '10_years'
       title text NOT NULL,              -- 'HVAC Filter Replacement'
       description text,                 -- 'Check monthly, especially if you 
     have pets; replace every 1–3 months.'
       interval_days int NOT NULL,       -- 30, 90, 180, 365, etc.
       alert_days_before int DEFAULT 14, -- Send alert this many days before due
       priority text DEFAULT 'Medium',   -- Low, Medium, High
       is_active boolean DEFAULT true,
       created_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()
     );

     Seed with Scott's maintenance items:
     - Monthly (30 days): HVAC Filters, Range Hood Filter, Disposal & Drains,
     Smoke/CO Detectors
     - Quarterly (90 days): Water Softener, Refrigerator Coils, Gutter Check
     - Biannual (180 days): HVAC Service, Gutter Cleaning, Deep Clean Windows,
     Exterior Faucets
     - Annual (365 days): Water Heater Flush, Dryer Vent, Septic Tank Check,
     Garage Door
     - 3-5 Years (1095 days): Roof Inspection, Exterior Paint/Stain, Chimney
     Sweep
     - 10 Years (3650 days): Replace Detectors, Check Appliances

     Step 2: Database — property_maintenance table

     Tracks which maintenance schedules are active for each property and when
     they're next due.

     CREATE TABLE public.property_maintenance (
       id serial PRIMARY KEY,
       property_id int NOT NULL REFERENCES properties(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
       schedule_id int NOT NULL REFERENCES maintenance_schedules(id),
       started_at timestamptz NOT NULL,    -- When the clock started (property 
     claim time)
       next_due_at timestamptz NOT NULL,   -- Next due date
       last_completed_at timestamptz,      -- When last marked complete
       last_task_id int REFERENCES to_do(id), -- Link to the current active task
       last_alerted_at timestamptz,        -- When last email was sent (prevent 
     duplicates)
       is_active boolean DEFAULT true,
       created_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()
     );

     Step 3: Auto-assign schedules on property claim

     File: app/api/claim-property/route.ts

     After the existing homeowner_id assignment, add:

     // After property is claimed:
     1. Fetch all active maintenance_schedules
     2. For each schedule:
        - Calculate first due date: started_at + interval_days
        - Insert into property_maintenance
     3. Log: "Assigned X maintenance schedules to property Y"

     Step 4: Extend daily cron — check for upcoming maintenance

     File: app/api/cron/tasks/route.ts

     Add a 4th step to the existing cron:

     4. Process Maintenance Alerts
        ├─ Find property_maintenance WHERE next_due_at <= today +
     alert_days_before
        │   AND (last_alerted_at IS NULL OR last_alerted_at < next_due_at -
     alert_days_before)
        ├─ For each:
        │   ├─ Create task for homeowner (if not already created)
        │   ├─ Send email alert
        │   ├─ Create in-app notification
        │   └─ Update last_alerted_at
        └─ Returns: { alerts_sent, tasks_created }

     Step 5: Maintenance alert service

     New file: lib/services/maintenanceAlerts.ts

     // Core functions:
     assignMaintenanceSchedules(propertyId, startedAt)
       → Inserts all active schedules into property_maintenance

     processMaintenanceAlerts()
       → Called by cron. Finds upcoming due items, creates tasks, sends emails.

     completeMaintenanceTask(propertyMaintenanceId)
       → Updates last_completed_at, calculates next_due_at, creates next task
     cycle

     getPropertyMaintenanceStatus(propertyId)
       → Returns all maintenance items with status for dashboard display

     Step 6: Email template for maintenance alerts

     File: lib/services/emailNotifications.ts

     Add new notification type 'maintenance_alert' with template:

     Subject: Flannel Maintenance Reminder — {title}

     You have been in your new home for {months} months and your {title}
     maintenance task has been assigned.

     {description}

     This needs to be completed in the next {days_until_due} days to stay
     on schedule with the manufacturer's recommendation.

     [View Task →] (link to /tasks)

     Step 7: SQL migration file

     New file: sql/MAINTENANCE_ALERTS_SETUP.sql

     Contains:
     - maintenance_schedules table + seed data
     - property_maintenance table
     - RLS policies (homeowner sees own property's maintenance, builder sees
     their properties)
     - Indexes on property_id, next_due_at

● The one we've been using is onxmnhunyzytztkuerhg — that's the test instance

  The other one (tnkcuhyubwaumgpequfq) is the live/production instance. Don't

❯ so  if we do  migration in one how the live  will pick  the changes??         

● Good question. When it's time to go live, you run the same SQL migration on
  the production instance (tnkcuhyubwaumgpequfq) too. Supabase doesn't sync
  schemas between projects automatically.

  The workflow is:
  1. Test instance — run migration now, test everything
  2. Production instance — run the same MAINTENANCE_ALERTS_SETUP.sql when you're
   ready to deploy

  The code changes (PR merge) deploy to Vercel automatically, but the database
  schema changes need to be run manually on each Supabase instance.

  So for now, run it on the test one (onxmnhunyzytztkuerhg). We'll run it on
  production later after testing.

❯ does the previous migration we did fixing the 2 bugs are those  in  prod how  
did they  gone to pod??                                                         

● The SQL migrations we created (BACKFILL_CHUNK_METADATA.sql) were only run on
  the test instance — we ran them manually via Node.js scripts. They are NOT on
  production.

  The code went to production when we merged to main (Vercel auto-deploys from
  main). But here's the problem:

  Production is likely broken right now because:
  1. The code now uses Gemini for embeddings, but production's GOOGLE_AI_API_KEY
   probably isn't set in Vercel env vars
  2. Production database still has OpenAI embeddings — incompatible with Gemini
  query embeddings
  3. The BACKFILL_CHUNK_METADATA.sql was never run on production
  4. The match_documents_by_source function doesn't exist on production

  To fix production, you need to:

● You're 100% right. Right now the maintenance schedules are hardcoded generic 
  items (HVAC every 30 days, water heater annually, etc.). But the real value is
   extracting maintenance schedules from the actual uploaded documents — because
   every product has different recommendations.

  For example, when someone uploads a Samsung washer manual, the system should:
  1. Extract "clean the filter every 2 months" from the manual
  2. Extract "run tub clean cycle monthly"
  3. Auto-create maintenance schedules specific to THAT product

  This is way better than generic schedules because it's personalized to what
  the homeowner actually owns.

  The flow should be:
  1. Homeowner uploads a product manual
  2. During processing, LLM extracts maintenance recommendations from the
  document
  3. Custom maintenance schedules created for that property based on the actual
  document content
  4. Alerts sent based on the manufacturer's real recommendations

  Want me to build this? It would add a step in process-pdf that uses Gemini to
  extract maintenance items from the document and creates property_maintenance
  entries automatically. The generic seed data becomes a fallback for when no
  documents are uploaded.

 and its needs to be dynamic not hardcoded cause  we are going to  serve        
customers think from first principle... I gave you examples to see...

          +params: {

      388 +  const { markdownContent, documentTitle, documentId, property
          +Id, homeownerId } = params;                                   
      389 +  const errors: string[] = [];                                
      390 +                                                              
      391 +  if (!process.env.GOOGLE_AI_API_KEY) {                       
      392 +    return { extracted: 0, errors: ["GOOGLE_AI_API_KEY not set
          +"] };                                                         
      393 +  }                                                           
      394 +                                                              
      395 +  try {

      398 +    // Use Gemini to extract maintenance items from the docume
          +nt                                                            
      399 +    const result = await ai.models.generateContent({          
      400 +      model: "gemini-3-flash-preview",

      402 +        parts: [{

      404 +                                                              
      405 +For each maintenance item found, return a JSON object with:   
      406 +- title: Short task name (e.g. "Clean Lint Filter")           
      407 +- description: What to do and why, using the document's exact 
          +wording                                                       
      408 +- interval_days: How often (30=monthly, 90=quarterly, 180=bian
          +nual, 365=annual)

          +as_needed"                                                    
      410 +- priority: "Low", "Medium", or "High" based on safety/importa
          +nce                                                           
      411 +                                                              
      412 +Also extract warranty information:                            
      413 +- Warranty period (how long coverage lasts)                   
      414 +- What's covered and what's excluded                          
      415 +- Any registration requirements                               
      416 +                                                              
      417 +Return ONLY a JSON object:                                    
      418 +{                                                             
      419 +  "maintenance_items": [...],                                 
      420 +  "warranty": {                                               
      421 +    "duration_description": "...",                            
      422 +    "coverage_summary": "...",                                
      423 +    "expiry_interval_days": 365                               
      424 +  },                                                          
      425 +  "product_name": "...",                                      
      426 +  "product_type": "..."                                       
      427 +}                                                             
      428 +                                                              
      429 +If NO maintenance items are found, return {"maintenance_items"
          +: [], "warranty": null, "product_name": "", "product_type": ""
          +}                                                             
      430 +                                                              
      431 +DOCUMENT TITLE: ${documentTitle}                              
      432 +                                                              
      433 +DOCUMENT CONTENT (first 15000 chars):                         
      434 +${markdownContent.substring(0, 15000)}`                       
      435 +        }],                                                   
      436 +      }],                                                     
      437 +    });                                                       
      438 +                                                              
      439 +    const responseText = result.text || '';                   
      440 +    const jsonMatch = responseText.match(/\{[\s\S]*\}/);      
      441 +                                                              
      442 +    if (!jsonMatch) {                                         
      443 +      console.log("[maintenance] No JSON found in Gemini extra
          +ction response");                                             
      444 +      return { extracted: 0, errors: [] };                    
      445 +    }                                                         
      446 +                                                              
      447 +    const parsed = JSON.parse(jsonMatch[0]);                  
      448 +    const items: ExtractedMaintenanceItem[] = parsed.maintenan
          +ce_items || [];                                               
      449 +                                                              
      450 +    if (items.length === 0) {                                 
      451 +      console.log(`[maintenance] No maintenance items found in
          + "${documentTitle}"`);                                        
      452 +      return { extracted: 0, errors: [] };                    
      453 +    }                                                         
      454 +                                                              
      455 +    console.log(`[maintenance] Extracted ${items.length} maint
          +enance items from "${documentTitle}"`);                       
      456 +                                                              
      457 +    const supabase = getSupabaseAdmin();                      
      458 +    const now = new Date();                                   
      459 +    let created = 0;                                          
      460 +                                                              
      461 +    for (const item of items) {                               
      462 +      try {                                                   
      463 +        // Create a custom maintenance schedule for this docum
          +ent                                                           
      464 +        const { data: schedule, error: schedErr } = await supa
          +base                                                          
      465 +          .from("maintenance_schedules")                      
      466 +          .insert({                                           
      467 +            category: item.category || "as_needed",           
      468 +            title: `${item.title} (${documentTitle})`,        
      469 +            description: item.description,                    
      470 +            interval_days: item.interval_days || 365,         
      471 +            alert_days_before: item.interval_days <= 30 ? 7 : 
          +item.interval_days <= 90 ? 14 : 30,                           
      472 +            priority: item.priority || "Medium",              
      473 +            is_active: true,                                  
      474 +          })                                                  
      475 +          .select("id")                                       
      476 +          .single();                                          
      477 +                                                              
      478 +        if (schedErr || !schedule) {                          
      479 +          errors.push(`Failed to create schedule "${item.title
          +}": ${schedErr?.message}`);                                   
      480 +          continue;                                           
      481 +        }                                                     
      482 +                                                              
      483 +        // Assign to the property                             
      484 +        const { error: pmErr } = await supabase               
      485 +          .from("property_maintenance")                       
      486 +          .insert({                                           
      487 +            property_id: propertyId,

      489 +            homeowner_id: homeownerId,

          +l_days * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000).toISOString(),                  
      492 +            is_active: true,                                  
      493 +          });                                                 
      494 +                                                              
      495 +        if (pmErr) {                                          
      496 +          errors.push(`Failed to assign "${item.title}" to pro
          +perty: ${pmErr.message}`);                                    
      497 +        } else {                                              
      498 +          created++;                                          
      499 +          console.log(`[maintenance] Created: "${item.title}" 
          +(every ${item.interval_days} days, ${item.priority})`);       
      500 +        }                                                     
      501 +      } catch (itemErr: any) {                                
      502 +        errors.push(`Error processing "${item.title}": ${itemE
          +rr.message}`);                                                
      503 +      }                                                       
      504 +    }                                                         
      505 +                                                              
      506 +    // Store warranty info in document metadata if found      
      507 +    if (parsed.warranty) {                                    
      508 +      try {                                                   
      509 +        const { data: existing } = await supabase             
      510 +          .from("documents_metadata")                         
      511 +          .select("metadata")                                 
      512 +          .eq("id", documentId)                               
      513 +          .single();                                          
      514 +                                                              
      515 +        await supabase                                        
      516 +          .from("documents_metadata")                         
      517 +          .update({                                           
      518 +            metadata: {                                       
      519 +              ...(existing?.metadata || {}),                  
      520 +              warranty_info: parsed.warranty,                 
      521 +              product_name: parsed.product_name,              
      522 +              product_type: parsed.product_type,              
      523 +            },                                                
      524 +          })                                                  
      525 +          .eq("id", documentId);                              
      526 +                                                              
      527 +        console.log(`[maintenance] Stored warranty info for "$
          +{documentTitle}"`);                                           
      528 +      } catch (wErr: any) {                                   
      529 +        errors.push(`Failed to store warranty info: ${wErr.mes
          +sage}`);                                                      
      530 +      }                                                       
      531 +    }                                                         
      532 +                                                              
      533 +    return { extracted: created, errors };                    
      534 +  } catch (err: any) {

          + err.message);

      537 +  }

  1. Upload a document → text extracted + embedded
  2. Gemini analyzes the document → extracts maintenance items (filter changes,

  Hey Scott, here's the maintenance alert system in action:
  https://www.loom.com/share/93e7793e488d4e2e88b0775c8c2f74d6

  When a document is uploaded, the system reads it and automatically extracts
  all recurring maintenance tasks from the manufacturer's recommendations. Tasks
   show up immediately with their due dates so the homeowner always knows what's
   coming up. As due dates approach, priority escalates automatically and email
  reminders go out. Once a task is completed, the next cycle gets scheduled.
  Everything is dynamic — extracted from the actual product documents, not
  hardcoded.

● Here's the full audit:

  Current Upload System — Issues Found

  Issue: 5-file limit
  Where: UploadDropzone.tsx:229
  Current: MAX_UPLOAD_FILES = 5
  Problem: Arbitrary limit, no reason for it
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: No folder upload
  Where: Upload modal
  Current: Single files only
  Problem: Can't upload a folder structure
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: PDF only
  Where: UploadDropzone.tsx:250-253
  Current: Only .pdf accepted
  Problem: Storage bucket allows images + text too
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: Flat folder structure
  Where: documents_metadata.folder
  Current: Single-level folders
  Problem: No nested folder support
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: Sequential processing
  Where: Upload modal
  Current: Files processed one by one
  Problem: Slow for many files

  The 5-file limit is hardcoded at UploadDropzone.tsx line 229:
  export const MAX_UPLOAD_FILES = 5;

  And the folder system is flat — just a single string field folder in
  documents_metadata. No nested folders like Appliances/Kitchen/Dishwasher.

  What the user needs:
  1. Remove the arbitrary file limit
  2. Folder/directory upload — drag a whole folder, preserve the structure
  3. Nested folder support — folder field stores the full path

  Want me to build this? The changes would be:
  - Remove MAX_UPLOAD_FILES limit (or set to something high like 100)
  - Add folder upload support to the dropzone (HTML5 webkitdirectory attribute)
  - Preserve folder structure from the uploaded directory
  - Process files in parallel batches for speed

● Right. Let me think about this properly before coding.

  Folder structure metadata — yes, we need to store the full folder path so the
  system knows the hierarchy. Currently folder is a flat string like "Kitchen".
  It should support "Appliances/Kitchen/Dishwasher".

  Chat service impact — the concern is:
  1. More documents = more chunks = slower retrieval? No — our routing already
  narrows to the right document before search
  2. Folder structure should be available to the chat — so when user asks "show
  me my kitchen appliance docs", the AI can find them by folder path
  3. The processing pipeline (Gemini embedding, maintenance extraction) runs
  per-file, so more files just means more processing time at upload, not slower
  chat

  The changes needed:
  1. Remove MAX_UPLOAD_FILES limit
  2. Add folder upload support (preserve directory structure)
  3. Store full folder path in documents_metadata.folder (e.g.
  "Appliances/Kitchen")
  4. Make the document routing aware of folder structure (so chat can find docs
  by location)
  5. Process files in parallel batches to keep upload fast

  No schema changes needed — folder is already a text field, it just stores
  deeper paths now.

  Want me to proceed with building this?
